What's on in Cambridge — 15 Oct 2026editor's version

Events 15 Oct – 25 Oct · weekends: 17–18 Oct and 24–25 Oct

Two weeks of big names at the Corn Exchange (Nish Kumar, Michael Palin, Maisie Adam all play within the same fortnight), new exhibitions opening at the Fitzwilliam including Frank Bowling and Pre-Raphaelite works, half-term Halloween trails at Wicken Fen and Audley End House, and Cambridge United at home twice.

Theme of the week: New seasons open at the Fitzwilliam

Over this fortnight the Fitzwilliam opens two new shows — An Enamel Revolution on 20 October and A Call to Art on 23 October — alongside its big Frank Bowling retrospective and the Mughal Songbook exhibition already running.

Frank Bowling: Seeking the Sublime

until 17 Jan 2027 · Fitzwilliam Museum, Cambridge · free

Nearly seven decades of the British painter's work, from his figurative paintings of the 1960s to the large abstract canvases he makes today, tracing the influence of Titian, Turner and Tracey Emin on his painting.

Why go: Free entry to a show covering nearly 70 years of one artist's career.

More →

An Enamel Revolution: China and the World

20 Oct – 21 Feb 2027 · Fitzwilliam Museum, Cambridge · free

How Chinese workshops from the 1600s revolutionised enamel decoration on porcelain, glass and copper, including pieces adapted for export to European buyers.

More →

A Call to Art: William Morris and the Pre-Raphaelites

23 Oct – 3 May 2027 · Fitzwilliam Museum, Cambridge · from £16, free for members

Morris and the Pre-Raphaelites' designs, textiles and paintings, drawn from the museum's own collection, built around their belief that art could reshape society.

More →

A Mughal Songbook: Art, Music and Empire

until 7 Mar 2027 · Fitzwilliam Museum, Cambridge · free

The rare Plowden manuscript of 77 Persian and Urdu songs from Mughal South Asia, around 1750–1850, shown alongside illustrations and sound.

More →

The weekend: 17–18 Oct

Nish Kumar: Angry Humour From a Really Nice Guy

Sat 17 Oct, 10:00 · Cambridge Corn Exchange, Cambridge · £28

Nish Kumar brings his sharp stand-up to Cambridge Corn Exchange, taking aim at widening inequality, rising autocracy and general political chaos. Expect the mix of fury and warmth that made him a Mash Report favourite, packed into an hour that's as pointed as it is funny.

More →

The Beat: Dance Crazed Too

Sun 18 Oct · Cambridge Corn Exchange, Cambridge · £43–£45.20

The 2-Tone band led by Dave Wakeling, formed in 1979, plays a 21-date tour of their ska classics including Mirror in the Bathroom and Save It for Later.

More →

WCB Comedy: Hal Cruttenden, Reginald D Hunter and Dan Antopolski

Sat 17 Oct, 19:00 · The Great Hall at The Leys, Cambridge · from £26

Three stand-ups share one bill: Hal Cruttenden, Reginald D Hunter and Dan Antopolski.

More →

Apple Day 2026

Sun 18 Oct, 10:00 · Cambridge University Botanic Garden, Cambridge · £7.20–£13.80, under-16s free

The region's biggest Apple Day, with apple tasting, identification and family activities across the Garden.

More →

Pasadena Roof Orchestra

Sun 18 Oct, 15:30 · Saffron Hall, Saffron Walden · within 30 min · £17

A celebration of 1920s and '30s dance-band music — Gatsby-era Cotton Club jazz played on period-style orchestrations.

More →

Creative Street Photography Talk with Matt Hall

Sun 18 Oct, 13:30 · Corpus Christi College, Cambridge · £5

Photographer Matt Hall talks about creative approaches to street photography, including fine art techniques and abstraction, as part of the CPS 26 talks series.

More →

The weekend: 24–25 Oct

Maisie Adam: Whatsherface

Sun 25 Oct, 10:00 · Cambridge Corn Exchange, Cambridge · £28

Straight off Taskmaster and A League of Their Own, Maisie Adam lands at Cambridge Corn Exchange with a brand new hour called Whatsherface. Expect rapid-fire gags, plenty of chaos and maybe a few angry outbursts from the comic once dubbed 'the tall, chaotic one with weird hair'.

More →

London Philharmonic Orchestra

Sat 24 Oct, 19:30 · Saffron Hall, Saffron Walden · within 30 min · from £28

Conductor Edward Gardner leads the LPO in Tchaikovsky's symphonic portrait of Byron's brooding hero Manfred.

More →

Black Slate

Sun 25 Oct, 19:00 · Cambridge Junction, Cambridge · £28

The reggae band formed in London in 1972, known for their 1976 hit Sticksman, play live.

More →

Space Safari Drone Light Show

Sat 24 Oct, from 16:00, show about 19:15–20:00 · Newmarket Racecourse, Newmarket · within 30 min · prices on the website

Hundreds of synchronised LED drones turn the night sky into animals and space scenes, narrated by a TV presenter.

More →

Halloween Half-Term at Audley End House and Gardens

24 Oct – 1 Nov · Audley End House and Gardens, Saffron Walden · within 30 min · included in admission

Half-term Halloween activities for families around the historic house and gardens, included in the admission price.

More →

Cambridge United FC v Plymouth Argyle

Sat 24 Oct, 15:00 · Abbey Stadium, Cambridge · prices on the website

Home league match for Cambridge United, who play Plymouth Argyle in League One this season.

More →

Clare Hall's Ceramics Symposium

Sat 24 Oct · Clare Hall, Cambridge · prices on the website

A day-long symposium on studio ceramics, bringing together scholars, curators and practitioners to look at the international influences on 20th-century studio pottery, alongside the launch of the catalogue for the Bell Collection.

More →

Cambridge Film Festival 2026

22 Oct – 1 Nov · Cambridge Arts Picturehouse and partner venues, Cambridge · prices on the website

The festival marks its 45th edition with 11 days of cinema from more than 25 countries, including Palme d'Or winner Fjord, Martin McDonagh's Wild Horse Nine, Jesse Eisenberg's The Debut and the British drama I See Buildings Fall Like Lightning, plus filmmaker Q&As and a free Family Film Festival strand.

Why go: 45th edition of the festival, with almost 100 films from more than 25 countries.

New in town

The Pickerel Inn

Recently opened · Cambridge

A historic pub founded in 1608 has reopened after refurbishment with a new all-weather courtyard.

Why go: Founded in 1608.

More →

UNIQLO

Opened 24 Sep · Grand Arcade, St Andrew's Street, Cambridge

The Japanese clothing brand's first Cambridge store spans two floors of the Grand Arcade, with an exclusive collection made with the University of Cambridge.

Why go: First UNIQLO store in Cambridge.

More →

Catte Latte

Opened in Aug · Mill Road, Cambridge

Cambridge's first cat café, with timed one-hour visits bookable online for £14.49 on weekdays and £17.99 at weekends.

More →

Tripots

Opened in Sep · Quayside, Cambridge

A new hot-pot restaurant where each table cooks its own meal from a choice of ingredients, in a bright, modern space with river views.

More →

Bridge Bagels

Opened 28 Sep · Cambridge

Hand-rolled, kettle-boiled New York-style bagels, baked fresh with locally sourced ingredients, ordered online for collection.

More →

Arbury Social

Opened 25 Sep · Meadows Community Centre, Arbury Road, Cambridge

A new community café has opened at the Meadows Community Centre on Arbury Road, cooking freshly made meals from seasonal, surplus and locally sourced ingredients. It's run on a not-for-profit basis by Cambridge Sustainable Food CIC, in partnership with the city council, with the aim of cutting food waste and keeping meals affordable.

Why go: Run on a not-for-profit basis by Cambridge Sustainable Food CIC with the city council.

More →
